ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u8	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of the draft law on amending the Law on healthcare services financed by public funds and certain other laws, as stated in the explanatory memorandum, is to optimise the expenditure of the National Health Fund on guaranteed healthcare provision and to standardise benefits. This objective will be achieved by the right of the Minister of Health to change medical technology, which will allow clarification of the content of the list of guaranteed benefits. Benefits of failed clinical efficacy and undocumented safety, which generate high costs on the part of the public payer, will be identified. The scope of individual guaranteed benefits will be adjusted, including by amending certain rules on the refund of medicines, special purpose foodstuffs and medical devices. At present, the Ministry of Health does not have the right conditions... [[Mr. Marshal, will you calm...]] [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] ...necessary to carry out a change in medical technology from the perspective of the clinical relevance of funding...

Funding from public guaranteed benefits with undocumented effectiveness and inefficient benefits cost-effectively exposes the public payer to unreasonable spending. At the same time, the project introduces a solution to provide patients with access to medicines which are not financed by public funds and are necessary to save the life or health of the recipient, and where justified and supported by indications of current medical knowledge. This is necessary because there are situations in the current legal state where it is not possible for a patient to be given a medicine of proven effectiveness, [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] because it is not a guaranteed witness while it can save the patient's life. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u12	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The main objective of the draft law on amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds, i.e. the so-called. The emergency access to medical technologies, emergency technologies, is to optimise and increase transparency in the expenditure of the National Health Fund on guaranteed healthcare provision and standardise the implementation of these benefits. These are two priorities that we are trying to achieve in health care: transparency of expenditure, so that no spent zloty raises the slightest doubts about its validity, and well understood standardisation, so that the various technologies and benefits are valued according to clear keys and objective criteria. Funding from public guaranteed benefits with poor efficiency and inefficient benefits cost-effectively exposes the public payer - as has already been mentioned - to irrational public spending. The Polish state cannot afford to pay, often extremely expensive, benefits that are not effective. We are all well aware of many problems of Polish health care. One of them is insufficient. That is why we need to take care of effective and effective treatment. We can't spend money lightly. The project introduces solutions to provide patients with access to medicines which are not financed by public funds in a given indication and which are necessary to save the life or health of the recipient. This is necessary because there are situations in the current legal situation where it is impossible to give the patient a cost effective medicine of proven effectiveness because it is not a guaranteed provision while it can save the patient's life. It is time to correct the mistakes of the predecessors who have led to a condition where the patient is merely a statistic, a position in the balance sheet, and human life is less important than a procedure. The implementation of the project will have a significant impact on citizens' health. This will result from the use of proven effectiveness and restriction of the use of technologies that do not meet the requirements of the assessment of the drug technology as part of the benefits of guaranteed medical technologies. The Act's long-term actions will reduce the negative consequences of the use of inefficient medical technologies, including hospitalisation and the need to treat complications, as well as the lack of ability to work or to limit them. The bill provides for the possibility for the Minister of Health to issue a decision on a partial refund of a drug not commercially available on the territory of the country and imported from abroad under the conditions enshrined in the Pharmaceutical Law. A lot has been said recently about importing drugs from abroad and their availability, about the real need for such action. Much of the medicines needed for effective treatment of Polish patients are available, but there are exceptions to this rule. There are real difficulties in getting some drugs that - although nominally available - cannot be obtained in practice. This is the answer to this project. Thanks to this law, we will eliminate even a few cases when you have difficulty getting medicines. No one will be left alone. Allowing the Agency to issue recommendations will provide patients with access to benefits, first, the most important in terms of protecting the health of society, secondly, the proven clinical efficiency, thirdly, the most cost-effective of delivery and, fourthly, adapted to the financial possibilities of the National Health Fund. These are real benefits for the patient. We don't promise the golden mountains. We promise reliable valuations, recommendations based on expert knowledge, and thus - the choice of the best treatment methods and tools for the patient. Public attention is also paid to the so-called issue. contraception the day after. Focusing on the content of the Act, on the real solutions that will be enshrined in the law, it should be pointed out that thanks to the above regulation the doctor will be able to assess whether the use of the medicine will not adversely affect the patient's health. This is a practice commonly used for hormonal drugs, which - not to know why - has been omitted here. This is not about ideological issues, but above all about the health of patients who are indirectly deprived of specialist care and reliable information. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u22	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws is a great process by which six major laws within the framework of this amendment will be amended, namely the Act of August 2004 on the provision of health care financed by public funds, the Act on the Refund of Medicines of 2011, the Act of August 1997 concerning the protection of personal data, the Act of 15 April 2011 on medicinal activities and the Act of September 2001 concerning pharmaceutical law. This is another fragmented model of the change of this system, which is a very difficult system, complex organizationally, financially, all the more so because we know perfectly from the announcements that since 1 January 2018. The National Health Fund will be liquidated, health funding from the state budget. Therefore, the designer assumes that benefits with failed clinical efficacy or unproven safety will be identified, which generate very high costs on the part of the payer. I think that these possibilities still exist and there is no need for statutory changes, especially since the explanatory memorandum does not state what is the estimated scale of these large benefits with failed clinical performance. The project also obliges providers to cooperate with the Agency in obtaining the necessary personal data free of charge to determine the service tariffs. The already General Data Protection Officer has objected to this project which gives the Health Minister an excessive power to collect and process data. The General Inspector considers that this provision is too broad and inadequate for data processing purposes. The project also included changes to the statute and validity of the entire system of the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies. From now on, it will be the Minister of Health who will approve this service tariff and, in particularly justified cases, will be able to change the service tariff without indicating in the explanatory memorandum, even in general, what the legislator means by the concept of justified cases. This will be another transfer from the Minister of Health to the administration of this agency. The proposal also introduces a mandate for the Minister of Health, which will personally shape the composition of the Transparency Council, the Tarification Council, which are the consultative bodies within the Agency for the President. Until now, the dismissals of these members of the councils have been registered by law, indicating a closed catalogue of cases in which they could be dismissed. From now on, it will be the Minister of Health who will assess individually whether a person can continue to be a member or whether he or she should be released. This amendment is due to take place on 30 June 2017. Besides, of course, this is a project that is designed to lead to emergency access to drug technology. Here again, it is necessary to clarify that this project provides that it is the Minister himself, at the request of the patient, who will be able to finance a given drug from public funds, which is not available on the list of medicines reimbursed. And here, of course, once again, we see a lack of transparency, transparency, which has been mentioned so much in the record. Within the framework of these six laws, which will be amended, it has also been tried to introduce into the Pharmaceutical Law a provision which will regulate the availability of medicinal products in the group of hormonal contraceptives for internal use. We know, of course, that this is about the so-called. emergency contraception available without a prescription, which is allowed in all countries of the European Union outside one country which has not benefited from this provision. I don't think it's hard to know that this country is Hungary. Even Malta, which is very restrictive in terms of contraception, introduced this medicine without a prescription. Of course, the legislator talks about excessive use by Polish patients of the consumption of these medicines. The main aim is to rule out the possibility of non-prescription use of emergency contraception. And finally, what your representative of the PiS club tried to tell you about, it's a matter of amendment. This applies to pharmaceutical technicians. The records so far meant that a few thousand people would lose their jobs because these restrictive provisions provided that pharmacy technicians would not be allowed to sell certain components, certain medicines. This involves protesting technicians and the possibility of losing jobs. Therefore, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club [[♪ The bell ♪]] it makes five amendments on matters that we disagree on. Today decisions are issued for a minimum of 2 years, but they can also be spent for 3 and 5 years. This regulation is widespread and depends on individual decisions of the Minister of Health the entire refund process in Poland, worth PLN 11 billion of public money. This will destabilize the system of reimbursement decisions, and negotiations with pharmaceutical companies will be conducted in a unclear way. Why do you not want to introduce a minimum period of reimbursement decisions in order to avoid the situation that they will be issued, for example? For a month? Don't you realize that by introducing this provision, you are violating the stability of the therapy of many sick people? However, this is not the end of controversial provisions of the bill. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u36	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds and certain other laws. This draft law deals with a number of changes that make it possible to treat patients individually in a particular health situation, which will significantly contribute to equalisation of the availability of treatment for people suffering from rare diseases. Among them there are among others. a procedure for emergency access to drug technologies to enable the Health Minister to grant individual authorisation to cover the costs of a medicine which is not financed by public funds. It is also possible to refund medicines imported from abroad necessary to save the patient's health or life, which are temporarily not available on the Polish market despite their admission to the market in Poland. In addition, the draft law introduces an obligation for medical institutions to provide the Agency for the Evaluation of Medical Technologies and Tarification of reliable and precise cost data, which are necessary to determine the tariff of benefits, and extends the competences of the Agency. According to the new provisions, the Minister of Health, on the basis of a recommendation from the President of the Agency, will be able to change the medical technologies used in the framework of guaranteed benefits so that patients have increased access to benefits with proven clinical efficiency. Most of the proposed changes to the regulation will contribute to improving the quality of treatment in Poland, support patients who have so far had to seek medical assistance on their own, and increase the effectiveness of spending money on treatment. However, I have reservations about the provisions proposed in the Refund Act concerning the period for which refund decisions are issued. The current rigid deadlines laid down in the Act are to be replaced by a flexible period of up to five years, which, according to the applicants, is expected to have a positive impact on the possibility of price negotiations and the adjustment to market developments. I have doubts that such a change will not disrupt the stability of the refund system so far. I am sorry to say that the work of the committee on this project has been dominated by one aspect concerning this amendment, i.e. the availability of the so-called. day after day. It is sad that for populist applause a number of other important solutions are overlooked. The revision we are looking at is a series of substantive changes of key importance for patients who have so far had limited funding opportunities for their treatment. It is worth putting aside the politically colored mantras that are constantly being delivered and focusing on constructive work. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceedings of the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u90	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It is my honor to present my position on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. The provisions of the government draft law amending the Act on the financing of the common agricultural policy from print No 1481 will contribute to the development of agricultural and forestry education. Thanks to the Act, local government units and state administrations running this type of school will gain access to money under the 2014-2020 guidelines for many activities covered by the transfer of knowledge, as well as information activities in this field. The most important activities covered by the Act are as important and improving the quality of training activities as those related to support for the development of vocational training in the field of agricultural and forestry activities, management, technology and organisation of production on the farm, including organic production, labour safety, marketing, accounting, farm insurance, the use of financial instruments, environmental protection and climate, including the use of renewable energy sources, the use of information and communication technologies, cooperative activities, the creation and functioning of producer groups, shortening the food chain. Under the sub-measures: support for demonstration projects and information activities will be provided for demonstration activities to promote and disseminate innovative solutions and good practices by enabling farmers or forest owners to familiarise themselves with solutions that have already been adapted and can be used in the agricultural, forestry or agri-food sectors. In particular, demonstration projects will be open to technology and organisation of production, processing of agricultural products produced on the farm and environmental and climate protection solutions. So they meet the challenges of civilization and increase the innovation of Polish farms. Selection of beneficiaries under the action: Knowledge transfer and information activities will take place transparently, through competitions for the preparation and implementation of training operations which will be announced separately for each sub-action, taking into account the principles of equal treatment and fair competition. The Law and Justice Club fully supports print project No 1481. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

About the mid-1990s, there has been a gradual decrease in the number of pupils in agricultural schools. First, the number of branches began to be reduced, transformed into other directions, and eventually schools in rural areas were liquidated. With the entry of Poland into the European Union and the emergence of the possibility to benefit from EU funds, agricultural education has again become very attractive. Today, a farmer is a profession that requires the right knowledge and skills. Polish farmers are energetic, imaginative people who, thanks to the experience of their parents and grandparents, solid education and a new approach to agriculture, are the drivers of the development of Polish agriculture. Therefore, ensuring the quality and level of knowledge is extremely important for the development of Polish agriculture, for preparing farmers for finding themselves on the national market and for meeting competition in European and global markets, and also, equally important, for gaining the ability to adapt flexibly to dynamically changing social, economic and economic conditions. According to the government, the entry into force of the proposed law will have a positive impact on the situation and regional development. With the possibility to make early use of EU and national resources allocated to the RDPs 2014,-2020, it will be possible to start the operation without having to wait for reimbursement of expenditure incurred from the paying agency. In my view, the law should not raise any doubts, and I would therefore like to state that Kukiz15 supports the government's proposed change. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u107	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 3 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the Commission's draft law amending the agricultural tax law and the Act on local taxes and charges (prints no. 1232 and 1507). I ask Mr Grzegorz Adam Wozniak to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u108	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report for print no. 1232 on behalf of the Joint Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy. It is a draft law on amending the agricultural tax law and the law on local taxes and charges. The contested provisions clarify the principle of joint and several liability for tax obligations where the subject-matter of the tax is in the joint ownership of persons, one or more of whom are exempt or not subject to tax. The Commission's proposals after work are as follows: the tax obligation should be borne jointly and severally only by those co-owners who are subject to tax and are not exempt from tax in so far as they correspond to their total share of ownership rights. The proposed amendment is neutral for the current legal situation and does not affect the level of tax burden. It is tidy and will therefore have a positive impact on taxpayers and tax authorities. Until now, the applicable law has referred to a tax obligation jointly and severally spread across all joint owners or holders. Such joint and several liability concerned both the entity, the tax liability of the debtors and the party concerned, i.e. the tax due. The judgments of the administrative courts showed the line that joint and several liability of the joint owners for tax obligations does not preclude the exercise of the statutory right to exemption from the tax obligation. The existence of a tax exemption should therefore result in this situation being taken into account in determining the amount of the tax liability. It cannot be that joint and several liability of joint owners for real estate tax obligations prevents the exercise of the statutory right to reduce the tax obligation. The joint committees ask the High House to adopt this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u109	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jerzy Gosievski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u110	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the Commission's draft law amending the agricultural tax Act and the Act on local taxes and charges, the original print No 1232. The combined report of the committees contained in print No 1507 extends the title of the law to the Forest Tax Act and therefore adds the article. 3 concerning the Forest Tax Act of 30 October 2002, which, according to the Government's proposal, has been supplemented by changes, similar to those mentioned in the two above, in terms of joint and several liability for tax obligations. The proposed amendments to the Agricultural Tax Act, the Local Taxes and Charges Act and the Forest Tax Act contain proposals which will eliminate doubts in the application of the law in such a way that the principle of joint and several joint ownership of real estate is limited where one or more joint owners are exempt from the obligation to pay agricultural tax, property tax or forestry tax. So far, some of the tax authorities, mostly municipalities, have misapplyed the applicable law. The proposed law contains a proposal in line with the current case law and the interpretation of the Ministry of Finance. Its essence is the introduction of a statutory standard according to which the principle of joint and several liability for agricultural tax or property tax obligations will not apply where one or more co-owners are exempt from payment of one of those taxes. In such a situation, the tax obligation will be jointly and severally imposed on joint owners not exempt from the obligation to pay the tax, and the amount of that obligation will correspond to the total share of those joint owners in the property right. The proposed law has no significant impact on public finances. There are cases in the country where co-owners pay property tax on the whole property usually occur when the amounts are small, because in such cases it is not profitable for the injured co-owner to refer the case to the court. The draft law removes a provision that is the source of many unnecessary court proceedings, thus extending the waiting period for judicial decisions in other cases. In view of the economic impact of the proposed regulation, particular attention should be paid to the fact that certainty in the way property is taxed is an important factor in the conclusion of a real estate purchase contract. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law on the amendment of the agricultural tax law, the law on local taxes and charges and the Forest Tax Act contained in print No 1507, which is a report of the joint committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u126	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Joint Committee on Energy and the Treasury and the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry a report on the draft law amending the Geological and Mining Law and some other laws, whose first reading took place on 20 April this year. The draft is contained in print No 1480 and the report of the joint committees in print No 1493. High Izbo! This revision of the Geological and Mining Law aims to implement into the Polish legal order Directive 2013/30/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 12 June 2013 on the safety of offshore oil and gas operations and amending Directive 2004/35/EC. The deadline for its implementation by the Member States of the European Union expired on 19 July 2015, and we therefore have to catch up on the decline after the previous government, and these discrediting omissions should be removed immediately. For this reason, the joint committees: the Committee on Energy and the Treasury and the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry immediately started working to prepare and report on the joint committees. High Izbo! Poland has limited own resources of oil and natural gas, so the activity associated with their exploration and extraction is not so strongly developed. The mining activity in the sea areas of the Republic of Poland in the case of crude oil results in about 160 t of annual extraction, for natural gas - about 17 million m3, and the concessions for exploration, recognition and extraction are held by the Lotos Petrobaltic SA Group. However, it is clear that all provisions of the directive mentioned at the outset should be implemented into Polish law, and the transposition of the provisions of the Directive into the Polish legal system aims to reduce the risk of a major accident in the pursuit of exploration, exploration and extraction activities for hydrocarbon deposits in marine areas by introducing mechanisms to protect the environment and improve the safety of the business. This will be done by strengthening and complementing solutions existing so far and currently in the Geological and Mining Law. The requirements referred to in the draft law concern: firstly, the pursuit of activities in the field of exploration, exploration and extraction of hydrocarbons in the maritime areas of the Republic of Poland in such a way as to ensure the safety and protection of the environment; secondly, the public's participation in the consultation of environmental impacts in this field; thirdly, the management of risks in terms of safety of persons and environmental pollution in both national and international cooperation in this field; fourthly, the supervision of the preparation of operators for the management of risks in terms of safety of persons and pollution of the environment in that activity; fifthly, liability for environmental damage and sixthly for disaster response. High Izbo! The current regulations in Geological and Mining Law do not require an operator operating in maritime areas to provide internal systems to prevent and respond to dangerous activities, and the system of supervision of the activities of mining plants does not provide for solutions that take full account of the specificities of activities in maritime areas. Therefore, the project imposes certain obligations on business operators. First of all, the entrepreneur should be able to fulfil the financial obligations arising from responsibility for activities related to the exploration, recognition and exploitation of hydrocarbons in the maritime areas of the Republic of Poland. The provisions of the Article shall apply. 49x and the following therefore provide for mandatory safeguards for claims which may arise from the activities carried out. Since the real risk of damage can only arise at the stage of the establishment of the mining plant, the establishment of the security will be followed by a decision approving the traffic plan of the mining plant, but before it is implemented. Each year the trader will have to submit to the concession authority an up-to-date proof of the existence of the security. High Izbo! The proposal introduces substantial changes to Article. 108.110 Geological and mining law concerning the movement of a mining plant. The provisions of the Geological and Mining Law relating to the mining plant's traffic plan to some extent correspond to the requirements of the Directive in Article. Article 11 and Article 11 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 12. They are now complemented by regulations on exploration, exploration and extraction of hydrocarbons from offshore sites. The existing provisions are supplemented by: firstly, the analysis of serious risks to the mining plant transmitted to the supervisory authority prior to the start of the implementation of the traffic plan, consulted with the representatives of the plant's staff and updated every 5 years; secondly, the description of the safety and environmental management system - the supervisory authority may require a review of the serious risks analysis by decision; thirdly, the description of the independent verification system carried out by the mining plant's motion assessor. The traffic plan should be accompanied by an expert's opinion on technical safety solutions. The independent verification system is the implementation of the Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 One, six and eight and Annex V of the Directive. The valuer should not be linked to the trader's business relationship and should not be involved in the creation of those solutions that will be verified by him. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 72 of the Geological and Mining Law was added point 22, which introduced a group of XXII rights of an expert on the traffic of a mining plant concerning the exploration, recognition and extraction of hydrocarbons from deposits within the maritime areas of the Republic of Poland. The powers will be conferred by a decision of the President of the Higher Mining Authority. In addition, the trader is required to record in such a way as to prevent the manipulation of the technical data of the operation carried out and to report to the mining supervisory authority on the implementation of the traffic plan. The scope of the report will be set out in the relevant Regulation. The rules introduced will allow for anonymous reporting of safety and environmental problems to mining supervisory authorities. And that's the rule that fills in the article. 22 Directives. The provisions on mining rescue are also being amended in order to comply with the requirements of the Directive. As regards the tasks of the competent authority, this law shall apply to the designer by implementing Article. Article 8 and Article 8 9 directives, entrusted tasks assigned to the competent authority within the meaning of the Directive to the President of the Higher Mining Authority. The President of the Higher Mining Authority shall be the central authority of the government responsible for mining supervision. High Izbo! The Geological and Mining Law provides for the separation of the powers of the President of the Higher Mining Authority from the tasks of other authorities of the State in relation to the management of natural resources in maritime areas, the granting of relevant concessions and the collection of funds for the pursuit of this activity. These legal and organisational arrangements make the President of the Higher Mining Authority a competent authority within the meaning of Article. The 8th Directive meets the requirements of independence and objectivity in the area of its competences arising from the implementation of the directive mentioned at the outset. High Izbo! The Act also catalogues the powers of the President of the Higher Mining Authority in the field of safety of operations, also defines the procedure for dealing with a dangerous incident or accident, cooperation with the European Commission in the implementation of reporting obligations and the forms and principles of cooperation with businesses and their employees in the development of standards and guidelines on best practices in this field. High Izbo! Each law must contain sanctions so that the rules laid down are respected. In the case of this project, it is assumed that failure by entrepreneurs and managers of the mining plant to comply with the obligations introduced by the Act will be subject to a financial penalty of up to 3% income of the punished entity achieved in the previous calendar year or up to 300% of the monthly salary of the head of the mining plant. The draft in print No 1480 and the draft in the committee's report also amend other laws: the Law of 21 March 1991 on the Maritime Areas of the Republic of Poland and the Maritime Administration, in which a supplement is added - a regulation imposing an obligation on the Director of the competent maritime office to establish a safety zone of at least 500 m wide around the mining plants operating in maritime areas. This draft law also amends the Law of 16 March 1995 on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships, the Law of 13 April 2007 on the Prevention and Repair of Environmental Damage and the Law of 18 August 2011 on Maritime Safety. High Izbo! The first case concerns the obligation to provide for safeguards for claims potentially arising from activities consisting in the exploration, exploration and exploitation of hydrocarbons within the limits of marine areas to be established by the trader at the establishment stage of the mining plant. And here is the first question that needs to be clarified: whether this regulation could lead to higher costs for the operation and a stronger position for large businesses, which could lead to less competition on the market - and therefore the question arises as to whether these were the objectives of the Directive. Further obligations under the Act include:: the obligation for an entrepreneur to establish and implement an independent verification system carried out by a mining plant traffic assessor, the obligation to ensure that authorised employees of the mining authorities transport to and from a mining plant situated within the limits of the maritime areas of the Republic of Poland and their accommodation and food, the obligation to develop and implement a system enabling the collection and recording of technical data relating to this activity, as well as the obligation to establish and implement an independent verification system carried out by the mining plant traffic assessor. And here there are further doubts: whether this regulation could lead to an increase in the costs of the operation, due to the large number of above mentioned. formal obligations relating to the adaptation of the company's activities to statutory requirements - which raises the question whether it is really necessary to increase formalism in the course of the activity carried out by the entrepreneur; whether the formal requirements indicated must indeed be introduced into Polish legislation; whether there is a market of experts in Poland prepared to carry out the assessments and analyses required by the amended regulations; whether the projector has an appropriate analysis of this market of experts. High Izbo! This amendment also introduces the principle of separating the concession award procedure from the implementation of the mining supervision of mining plants operating on the basis of those concessions and the separation of the competences of their operators. At the same time, the concession body was required to obtain the opinion of the President of the Higher Mining Authority in the course of the procedure for granting concessions for the exploration, recognition and extraction of hydrocarbons. The following doubts arise here: Do not the regulations described above contradict each other, in particular with regard to the principle of division of mining supervision in relation to the award of concessions, since the President of the Higher Mining Authority will participate in the concession award proceedings within the framework of its advisory powers? In addition, and if so, to what extent will the negative opinion of the President of the WUG affect the award of the concession and is it prepared in substance, formally and logistically to exercise his supervisory and opinional powers to an extended extent? High Izbo! Failure by traders and managers of the mining plant to comply with the obligations introduced by the Act will be subject to a financial penalty of up to 3% income of the punished entity achieved in the previous calendar year or up to 300% of the monthly salary of the traffic manager of the mining plant. The following questions arise here. Are the penalties described above justified in the light of the requirements of the Directive and are they proportionate, in particular with regard to the discretion of the President of the WUG with regard to the amount of the penalties? High Izbo! As I pointed out at the outset of my statement, this is only part of the doubts that should be clarified during the work of the subcommittee, which would probably discuss the details of the amendment of this law. The other institutions also have negative opinions on this law. These doubts could not be clarified, but the Civic Platform club would not be opposed to this amendment in view of the need to implement the directive. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u154	Dear Marshal, High Seym! I have the honour to present a report to the Joint Committee on Social and Family Policy, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Public Finance on a bill amending the Act on Pension Supply of Police Officers, Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Military Counterintelligence Service, Military Intelligence Service, Central Anti-corruption Office, Border Guard, Government Protection Office, State Fire and Prison Service and some other laws, print No 1521. The citizens' project entered the Sejm on 28 August 2015 and on 19 November was addressed to the first reading at the Sejm meeting held on 2 December 2015. The project was then addressed to three committees. During their joint meeting on 9 March 2016, the draft was addressed to the Emergency Sub-Committee. It held three sittings. A report was adopted at the meeting on 4 April, which was presented at the Joint Committee meeting on 9 May this year. In order to justify the draft law, the applicant identified as one of the main arguments the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 3 March 2015, which confirmed the claims of the environment of customs officers raised for years that they were discriminated against in the absence of the inclusion of Customs officers in the pension scheme applicable to other uniformed services. In the opinion of the applicants, the Customs Service is currently the only uniformed service not covered by the provisioning pension scheme for all uniformed services. The amendments tabled to the citizens' project were, in principle, a consequence of the solutions proposed in the government's position, as well as the inclusion in the supply system of the Customs and Treasury Service in connection with the adoption of the National Tax Administration Act and the creation of a new formation. The most reservations were raised by the amendment tabled in Article. 12, which assumes that a police pension is entitled to a duty-free officer who has 15 years of service in the said services on the day of his release, except for an official who has a fixed right to a pension as defined in the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act, calculated on the basis of periods of service and periods equivalent to that in the case of customs officers or customs officers, the completion of 55 years of age and having at least 5 years of service is additionally required: in the performance of the tasks referred to in Article. 1 paragraph 2 points 4 and 5 of the Customs Service Act of 24 July 1999 or Article. 2 (2) Point 1 of the Act of 27 August 2009 on Customs or Customs Services. In order to justify this change, the Minister stated that the scope of the pension rights for Customs officers proposed in the citizens' project was too far-reaching as it went beyond the limits resulting from the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. In order to implement that judgment, it is therefore necessary to identify a group of authorised officers. This amendment was accepted. Other amendments by the government concerned: the exclusion of the right to housing, as this formation does not have housing units; the withdrawal of the amendment proposed in the citizens' project providing for the establishment of the basis for the invalidity pension for Customs officers in the preparatory service in accordance with the rules provided for for officials in the candidate service, which is called. an alternative military service; the acceptance that the pension will be based on an officer's pension due for a further period of 10 calendar years chosen by an officer; the introduction of a regulation whereby a customs or customs-carboy officer who, on the day of his release from the service, conversion or termination of the service relationship, has reached a service term of 15 years, including at least 5 years in the performance of the so-called. Police tasks, but have not reached the age of 55, acquires the right to a pension at the date of age of that age; grant at the age of 55 the right to a police pension to a customs service officer who has reached the age of 25 on the date of his release but who has not reached the age of 55 years of age, and the exemption from the service has taken place in connection with the merger or abolition of organisational units subordinate to the minister responsible for public affairs; grant the right to a police pension to customs or customs-carb service officers who have been released from the service or whose service relationship has either been transformed or expired from 3 March 2015 until the date of entry into force of the law where those officials during the specified period fulfilled the conditions for the acquisition of the right to retirement in accordance with the procedure set out in the law on pension provision of uniform services; adopt the competence of the MSWiA pension authority to customs and customs-carb services. Part of the amendments was the consequence of the adoption that only the period of service from 15 September 1999 will be included in pension rights, and some regulations have become obsolete. 30 amendments were tabled at the Joint Committee meeting on 9 May. Most of them concerned a change in the scope of the Act by extending the circle of persons entitled to benefits from the supply system, changing the conditions necessary to obtain a police pension, adopting arrangements allowing customs and customs officers to be debubbed, introducing transitional arrangements for granting customs and sugar officers a 40% cash benefit the average salary of an officer in order to avoid a situation in which officers would be deprived of cash until the so-called. Police pensions, finally changing the date of entry into force of the Act. That date was set at 1 January 2018. The subject of the discussion was also the regulation on the inclusion of traineeships, services prior to 15 September 1999 in pension rights from the supply system, the establishment of a circle of officials entitled to provide pensions in the context of fulfilling the conditions for carrying out the tasks of the so-called. Police officers, the amount of the benefit paid until retirement from the supply system and the security of payment of cash benefits from the State budget. The committees accepted 21 amendments, 9 rejected. 33 Members voted in favour of the report, no one opposed it, with 4 abstentions. Importantly, the applicant's representative, the Chairman of the Legislative Initiative Committee, has assured that, although the amendments adopted do not fully meet their expectations, no amendments will be tabled at this stage of the procedure. In addition, he called on the parliamentary clubs not to submit any amendments which would lead to an extension of the legislative process and thus enable them to proceed with this project in the Senate as soon as possible. This draft law was long awaited by the environment's Customs officials, as the applicants stressed, and therefore, on behalf of the joint committees, I request that the High Sejm adopt the draft together with the amendments made. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Leaving the Customs Service outside the provisioning pension system causes many pathologies, e.g. That organized crime is being prosecuted by officers at the age of 60+. However, these facts cannot obscure another fact: unfortunately, the government's position has deviated from the social expectations expressed in the citizens' project and the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. Unfortunately, a compromise was not reached at yesterday's meeting of the three joint committees and, as a result, the current proposal only partially implements the judgment of the Constitutional Court, i.e. does not introduce full equality in the pension rights of Customs officers in relation to the officers of the other uniformed services, as the Constitutional Tribunal intended. This fragmented enforcement is due to the addition to the law at the legislative stage of conditions which fundamentally differentiate customs officers. First of all, there is an unfamiliar indication of the retirement age in other services, i.e. the age of 55 for officers adopted before 1 January 2013, while in any other uniform formation there is no indication of age for officers at all - there is only a reason for achieving 15 years of service. This is inappropriate because the Constitutional Tribunal in its operative part stated that such officers are discriminated against, precisely because they are not in the provisioning pension system. The implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal cannot therefore be referred to, at the same time adding only an additional condition of the age of 55 for them, a condition which is not present in other uniformed services. Secondly, a condition unknown to other uniformed services was introduced as a condition for the exercise of certain tasks as a condition for the acquisition of pension rights, while under the Customs Act it is uniformed, undivided, and uniformed uniformed uniforms, officers do not share according to the type of tasks performed. This does not allow them to do so because, in view of the professional availability of their duties and places of duty, they change repeatedly, even several times in the career of officers. For example, in the police nobody makes distinctions about the tasks performed for tasks more related to the fight against crime and the security of citizens and others - after all, not less important - tasks. He does not do so because he realizes that the specifics of the uniformed service - the Police, the Border Guard, but also the Customs Service - depended on and consists in the fact that the officer does not enter into any employment contract with the employer, as is the case with workers employed under the Labour Code. The officer is appointed to serve, ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo, and remains in an administrative service relationship with the Polish State, carrying out such tasks as the State will instruct him to do, but remaining fully available to carry out any task. At the same time, it has no influence on what tasks They will be commissioned to him and where he will do them. It is his duty to submit fully to the will of the employer representing the State. For these reasons, no one differentiates police officers or Border Guards. Why, then, should he, under the same conditions, have an administrative service relationship, full availability, rigors of service, constitutional restrictions on the right to make such a distinction only to customs officers? That is the question to be asked from this speaker. And I would ask you to lean on it when we conclude the compromise that we are hoping for during the committee's work, since the amendments have been tabled. Another legal problem is the specific closure of the eyes by the Sejm and the government for the fact that the officers have been carrying out tasks not since 15 September 1999, but they have done them much earlier, because the customs officers have carried out their tasks in uniform and on the basis of appointments - including prosecution and detection of criminal offences - since 1983. In the ministry there is daily stress, working in difficult conditions, especially at the border, where there are various cumbersome conditions, e.g. Carcinogenic exhaust gas and a real risk of loss of life and health. After all, there are known cases of assaulting officers, bombing a service vehicle, exactly a grenade, shooting a customs officer's car out of a machine gun, destroying private cars, private property officers by unknown perpetrators. All this in connection with the service rendered. Such situations existed before 1990, existed after it and will probably exist in the future, unfortunately. It is therefore justified to expect the Sejm and the government to recognise the nature of this work and to take this into account in the draft of this Act. We need a compromise, ladies and gentlemen. It should also be stressed that the Government's Legislative Centre, in an opinion issued after the Constitutional Court's judgment, indicated that different regulations from those in force in other uniformed services could be incompatible with the Constitution, and recommended that customs officers, today customs and sugar, be fully aligned with other servants' pension rights. The RCL opinion should send a signal to Members to reflect that their action, i.e. the possible passing of the law as it stands, without compromise, creates a legitimate risk of breaching the constitutional principle of equality by maintaining the state of discrimination. It is also acceptable here to recall the words of Minister Jarosław Zieliński made after the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal that the Customs Service will be fully aligned in its powers and that in the family of uniformed services it will finally take the right place, equivalent to other services. Also during the first reading of the project, in December 2015, in this Chamber from the mouth of Minister Jarosław Zieliński, I quote: If you accept the idea of splitting customs officers, you will soon have a legitimate view that the same applies. Maybe it should be the other services. Therefore, since until today the Council of Ministers and the Sejm have not undertaken any legislative work aimed at dividing police officers, Border Guards and other uniformed services on the basis of their tasks, the Sejm should not carry out such division only with respect to customs officers. Otherwise, Members, you may consciously accept the division of uniformed officers into the better ones, the first categories, and the worse, the weaker, the inferior categories, which deserve only the name of a poor relative unworthy to sit at a common table in a family of uniformed services. Those aren't just words. For behind them lies the history of every officer, both the police, the Border Guard and the Customs Service. Let's try to compare the story of a police officer who plays in the police orchestra, is a press spokesman or serves as a staff member. Although he never realized the so-called. Police duties, he's entitled to a uniform pension. Let's put this together with the history of a customs officer who had been leaking smuggling before that date for many years at the state border on 15 September 1999 and who, for example, had been involved in smuggling for many years. in May 2004, it was transferred to other tasks. This customs officer will not receive e.g. Uniform pension. Let's get these two stories together. The summary of these life situations is clear. Even intuitively, this example shows how flawed it is to divide uniformed officers. This should not be the case for a democratic legal state, but it should be reasonable, it should take care of constitutional standards, it should avoid discrimination. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u190	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the club Law and Justice I have the honour to present a position on print No.1324, a draft law amending the Act on Local Workers, which assumes the removal of provisions concerning political offices operating at the various levels of local government, at the entrance, at the mayor, city president, old age or the Marshal of the voivodeship. The project assumes that contracts with local government staff of political offices employed as advisers, assistants will be terminated as of the date of entry into force of this Act. In the current legal system, individual heads of government, mayors, presidents, starosts, marshals have the possibility to hire assistants and advisers on the basis of a contract of employment. However, the Act does not specify the tasks to be performed by persons employed in these posts. For the employment of assistants and advisers there is no obligation to apply the provisions on free recruitment, and until 2013, assistants, advisers employed by that time, could be transferred to other posts, including office posts, and there was often a lack of external recruitment. It is also worth noting that the remuneration rules for advisers and assistants are the same as for other local staff. The advisers and assistants shall be entitled to the remuneration appropriate to the position occupied and the professional qualifications held, including the basic salary, the allowance for the long-term work, the Jubilee Prize and all the associated privileges. The liquidation of political offices in local governments will reduce red tape and look for savings in the administration and thus respond to social expectations. The Law and Justice Club, the following project, supports and will work on it in committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u192	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I'm a little surprised at the Law and Justice position, but about that later. In the current project, there are no longer provisions relating to the liquidation of political offices in government structures, only the provisions relating to the recruitment of local staff as assistants and advisors are being repealed. The draft law proposes that the current law on local staff be removed from the current law in Art. 4 in paragraph 2. Point 2, i.e. the record of the posts of assistants and advisers, the deletion of Article. 17 on how to employ the above mentioned and on how to amend Article. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 1 - deletion from the list of posts laid down by the Council of Ministers' Regulation. Art. 2 of the proposed law assumes that employment contracts with currently employed advisers and assistants are terminated as of the date of entry into force of the law and Article. 3 sets the time limit for its entry into force. The Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy rejected the draft law by a large majority. The position of the Civic Platform Club is the same: we ask for the proposed bill to be rejected, we are in favour of the committee's position. According to our opinion, the bill is redundant, because the provision in the current law is optional and most local government units have not appointed any assistants or advisors at all. They're about 15%. municipalities, 14% the powiats and there are 57 posts in total in voivodship local governments. It is not true, as the applicants often say, that local governments employ incompetent persons in these positions. According to the Act, certain criteria relating to education and traineeships and to remuneration must be met. The advisor must have 5 years' work experience, higher education and appropriate competences, and an assistant to secondary education. Where such positions exist, they must be reflected in the budget of the commune, district or voivodeship approved by the councils of municipalities, powiats or seymikas. It should also be remembered that the number of assistants and advisers is limited depending on the size of the local government unit. During the adoption of the Act in 2008, as a result of a broad discussion, we decided to separate a group of employees who, with the governor or marshal, comes to the office and, after the end of the term of office, leaves with him. This is very clear. Local society, choosing a new council, district council or sejmik, or entrusting them with responsibilities again, also gives the possibility to select the closest employees and then the society will settle them in subsequent elections. The proposed bill also met with a negative opinion of local communities and organizations, including the Polish City Association and the Polish County Union. According to a survey conducted by the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, which took part in 1875 communes, 246 powiats and 15 voivodships, at municipal and district level only 14 10 of these voivodships have assistants, and 11 of them have advisers. The total expenditure for this purpose shall not exceed PLN 25 million. If we made such free conversions as the applicants sometimes do, it would turn out that the average expenditure on the employment of assistants and advisors in individual self-government units amounts to about 10,000. zł. That amount, I don't think, is shocking. The Local Workers Act is 10 years old, and I, as a Member who has been in close contact with local governments, have never heard of the need to liquidate these posts. As I said at the outset, the bill submitted is a piece of the previously processed and rejected, so why bring it back? Does this result from the silent agreement of the ruling party to hit local governments while leaving political offices in peace? We proceed with the examination of item 7 of the agenda: the report by the European Union's Committee on European Union Affairs on the Commission's draft resolution on the recognition of the draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal electricity market (recast) as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity (prints 1515 and 1524). ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u224	Mrs. Marshal! Dear Minister, The Commission for European Union Affairs at its 126th meeting on 21 April considered a draft Commission resolution of the Sejm containing a reasoned opinion on non-compliance with the subsidiarity principle of the proposal for a Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal electricity market. On 10 May this year. The committee carried out its first reading of the Commission's draft resolution. For the record. The assessment of the conformity of draft legislative acts of the European Union has become the law of national parliaments and an element of parliamentary diplomacy since the entry into force of the Treaty of Lisbon. National parliaments thus contribute to the proper functioning of the European Union. National Parliaments shall ensure that, in areas which do not fall under the exclusive competence of the European Union, action is taken only if, and only to that extent, the objectives of the intended action cannot be sufficiently achieved by the Member State at both central and regional and local levels and can be better achieved at Union level by reason of the scale or effects of the proposed action. It should be noted here that the draft regulation does not contain the required Treaty justification for the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality. Such justification should include a detailed statement enabling the assessment of compliance with the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality as well as an assessment of the financial implications of the European Union act concerned. The grounds for finding that the Union target can be better achieved at Union level should be justified on the basis of qualitative indicators and, where possible, quantitative indicators. In accordance with the Treaty provisions, any Chamber of the National Parliament may send a reasoned opinion to the Presidents of the European Parliament, the Council and the Commission within 8 weeks of the date of transmission of the draft legislative act containing the reasons why it considers that the draft is not in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity. That's when it comes to procedural issues. On the other hand, when referring to substantive matters, it must be stated that the reasoned opinion annexed to the draft resolution of the Sejm contains the reasons why the Sejm considers that the draft regulation does not comply with the principle of subsidiarity. Why? Firstly, it is considered unacceptable to take binding decisions at Union level in relation to the construction of cross-border connections which change the overall structure of energy supply from domestic production to imports. The need to pursue the Treaty objective of promoting cross-border mergers is not called into question, but this cannot be identified as taking decisions at Union level to build them and to oblige Member States to build them, irrespective of the economic efficiency and the state's decision on the overall energy supply structure. The proposed administrative means of forcing this process must be considered far-reaching. The subsidiarity principle is considered to infringe the proposed regulation on the possibility of applying measures at Union level affecting the structure of energy supply. These measures include, first, a ban on the construction of a dual-use market consisting of a power market and an energy market and the use of national energy resources in order to address the problem of insufficient resources at national level when the adequacy of resources at European level and the need for a priority rather than equivalent treatment of interconnection and demand-side response is identified. Secondly, the introduction of a CO2 limit of not more than 550 g CO2/kWh for the proposed support schemes for the construction of new generation capacities, power markets, and CO2 emission limits for entities participating in this market. This will lead to practical exclusion of the possibility of market share of coal power plants. Maintaining this limit will lead to the exclusion of the most economical and flexible measures to address the lack of generation capacity, which could ultimately result in an increase in energy poverty. Third, the requirement that resources derived from transmission congestion revenues be used to maintain interconnection capacity or to expand new interconnection capacity and that they cannot be used as income taken into account in the calculation of tariffs. In the case of a draft regulation, no formal requirements for compliance with the subsidiarity principle have been observed. The European Commission has not sufficiently justified that the objectives pursued by the proposed regulation cannot be better regulated at national level. The absence of such justification, in particular the lack of qualitative and quantitative indicators, constitutes an infringement of Article. 5 Protocol on the application of the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality annexed to the Treaty on European Union and to the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. The lack of such justification makes it very difficult if it does not prevent national parliaments from exercising their power to control respect for the principle of subsidiarity. Consequently, it may also be considered as a breach of Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 of the Treaty on European Union, according to which, in accordance with the principle of loyal cooperation, the Union and the Member States respect each other and assist each other in carrying out their tasks under the Treaties. High Izbo! The Polish Sejm and the Senate are not the only Chambers of Parliaments of the European Union which have considered adopting a reasoned opinion on this draft regulation contained in the document on the signature COM (2016) 861. As of 9 May this year. reasoned opinions were adopted by both chambers of the German Parliament, by the Hungarian Parliament, the Chamber of Members of the Czech Parliament and the Senate of the Romanian Parliament. At the same time, it should be stressed that the two successive parliaments - Danish and Lithuanian - despite the fact that the draft was compatible with the principle of subsidiarity, presented their opinions in the framework of the political dialogue containing a number of critical comments on the draft regulation. Poland also presented this opinion in the framework of the political dialogue with regard to the regulation on integrated national energy and climate plans. This concerns COM (2016) 759, 860. It should be recalled that on 15 December 2016, at the request of Prime Minister Beata Szydło, the conclusions of the European Council included a provision on the need to ensure freedom of action for Member States to ensure the necessary level of security of electricity supply at national level without discrimination against coal sources, which in some Member States are the cornerstone of energy security. The Member States of the European Union should have the means to ensure long-term capacity adequacy on the basis of certain stable and available energy sources. According to the Commission for European Union Affairs, limiting the sovereignty of the Member States of the European Union in choosing the necessary measures by introducing procedural, technological and economic barriers is incompatible with the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. In order to present the Polish argument and strengthen our opposition to the unfavourable solutions proposed in the draft regulation as President of the Parliamentary Commission for European Affairs, I have sent a letter to the chairmen of the committees for the European national parliaments of the Member States of the Union, in which I inform you of our reservations about the draft regulation. Similar actions have been taken by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. From direct discussions with the chairmen of the European Affairs Committee, among others: last in the framework of 7 and 8 May this year. meetings with committee presidents from Latvia, Lithuania and Estonia show that they support Polish reservations regarding the winter package, in particular regarding this regulation that we are talking about today. Polish opposition to our direct impact on our and regional energy security has been widely accepted to restrict the Treaty's right to independent shaping of its own energy mix. This is the position of Prime Minister Szydło at the European Council meeting on 16 December. The idea of introducing a emission threshold of 550 g CO2 /kWh, which mainly discriminates against Poland, has also been negatively assessed. The relatively short period of introduction of emission reductions was also critically assessed, which may mean for Poland that drastic changes in energy generation need to be made. The Commission requests the adoption of this draft resolution on the recognition of the draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal electricity market as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. Thank you very much.

Poland raises a number of significant objections to these proposals, which are aimed, in our view, primarily at weakening our energy security. Law and Justice precludes the proposed provisions from restricting the Treaty law of a Member State to the independent formation of an energy mix, firstly. Secondly, we oppose the introduction of the 550 g CO2/kWh benchmark. It is a discriminatory mechanism for countries such as Poland. Today we base our energy security on coal in almost 80%. We believe that the proposed provisions absolutely need to be modified to allow Member States to support the construction of new coal-based power plants. We believe that local electricity producers must have a level playing field on the European Union market, but also on external markets. The functioning of the electricity system should be based on market principles, without creating precisely artificial barriers such as emission thresholds. We do not agree that decarbonisation will become a specific religion of the European Union. It is also unacceptable that targets for renewable energy sources and energy efficiency are binding on Member States and that Member States need to design an investigation to them based on these objectives. At the same time, we believe that the recommendations made by the European Commission on the application of the objectives and measures and the path of access, as contained in the draft regulations, precisely: the recommendations must not be binding on the Member State. The European Commission must not go beyond the role provided for in the Treaties. The idea of a financial platform to be provided by Member States considered less ambitious by the European Commission to achieve its objectives is particularly opposed. This is unacceptable. We have recently met with the Chairs of the Committee on Foreign Affairs. The European Union of the Baltic countries and together we recognised that the decline in energy security in Poland will have a negative impact on the level of energy security in Lithuania, Latvia and Estonia. The issue of energy security for us is as important as it is for our neighbours in the Central European region. While, of course, appreciating the importance of cross-border energy cooperation, we oppose the creation of regional energy centres in place of national energy management centres, which have a specific responsibility. For these reasons, we believe that the Polish Parliament should adopt a reasoned opinion under the so-called procedure. the yellow card on the proposal for a regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal market in electricity, namely COM 861. The emission threshold proposed by the European Commission in the draft regulation as an energy source favours gas and eliminates coal-based technologies, including these modern technologies. In this way it interferes with national support mechanisms and effectively strikes the possibility of creating new energy sources based on national fuels in the Member States, in our case on coal. In our view, this is a clear violation of the rights guaranteed by the Treaties of a Member State and we will demand that the rights of a Member State be respected. Thank you very much.

According to the draft resolution of the Polish Parliament, the draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal market in electricity (recast) does not comply with the principle of subsidiarity referred to in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Treaty on European Union (TEU). According to the project promoters, this project violates the subsidiarity principle as the proposed regulation does not ensure that the objectives of the intended action at Union level are achieved in a better way than those of national action. In addition, the projectors argue that it is unacceptable at EU level to decide on the construction of cross-border connections that change the overall structure of energy supply from domestic production to imports. Without questioning the need to pursue the implementation referred to in Article. Paragraph 194. 2 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union (TFEU) the objective of promoting interconnection should be to ensure that this is not the same as taking decisions at Union level to build them and oblige Member States to build them, irrespective of the economic efficiency and the State's decision on the overall energy supply structure.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u249	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I present my position on the committee's report on the Commission's draft resolution on the recognition of the draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal electricity market as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. Since the beginning of our membership of the European Union, it has been stressed that the principle of subsidiarity is one of the guarantors of our sovereignty in relations with the European Parliament and the Council. It has been stressed that, in areas which do not fall under the exclusive competence of the European Union, the principle of subsidiarity upholds the decision-making capacity and action at Member State level and constitutes a legal justification for Union intervention if the objectives of the action cannot be sufficiently achieved by the Member States and can therefore, by reason of the scale or effects of the proposed action, be better achieved at Union level. The objective of introducing the principle into the European Treaties was therefore to exercise competence at the level as close as possible to the citizen, in accordance with the principle of proximity. The draft regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal market in electricity is incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. Member States are trying to dictate binding regulations for the construction of cross-border connections, which change the overall structure of energy supply from domestic production to imports. Poland is an importer of electricity, last year its consumption exceeded national production. In total, electricity imports covered the demand of about 1 million families. However, much more energy flows through our networks than we order. From Germany to Poland, electricity flows to the Czech Republic and Slovakia, then back to southern Germany and Austria, Slovenia or Italy. Germany has too poorly developed transmission networks to deliver all contracted energy from the north to the south of the country and to Austria, so energy flows through neighbouring networks as so-called. circular flows. Of course, we all agree that Poland should expand, modernise and modernise its infrastructure. However, the proposed resolution rightly points out that it is unacceptable for a binding Member State at EU level to decide on the construction of cross-border connections that change the overall structure of energy supply from domestic production to imports. The plans to create a common energy market are very ambitious. The Commission would like to move away from centralised large energy production blocks and rely on diffuse RES. Some time ago, a bold vision was presented to establish fair rules for the exchange of energy between countries that will strengthen competition and take into account the characteristics of national and regional markets. It is planned to establish compensation mechanisms for cross-border flows of electricity and harmonised rules for the shaping of cross-border transmission charges and the allocation of available capacity on cross-border lines. However, these plans are far from being implemented, but we already see today that the proposed administrative means of forcing this process are undemocratic and incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. Kukiz'15 recognises that the solutions contained in the EU document can be easily discussed and implemented at national level. We therefore support the resolution of the Sejm in its proposed form. The draft Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the internal market in electricity does not comply with the principle of subsidiarity. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Through our electricity grids much more energy is transmitted than we order, mostly electricity coming from Germany. I have questions: Do we bear additional costs? Does WW. Transport brings proportionate benefits? Please respond in writing. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u250	We will proceed to the vote on the draft resolution in the voting block.
