ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u251	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! On behalf of the Civic Platform Group, I have the honour to provide a justification for the proposed amendment to the Code of Administrative Procedure Act, print No 1499. High Seym! In the draft amendment of the Law of 14 June 1960, the Code of Administrative Procedure is to give the public administration the right to waive suspended proceedings in the event of the death of the party and its statutory representative or to lose the legal capacity of the party or the legal representative. The draft law provides for an extension of the possibility of winding up proceedings after the expiry of the three-year period of suspension of proceedings also in the event of other grounds for compulsory suspension of proceedings. Article 4 is replaced by the following: § 97 § 1 of the Code of Administrative Procedure requires the public administration body to suspend proceedings, inter alia. in the event of the death of a party or of one of the parties, where it is not possible to call on the heirs of the deceased party to participate in the proceedings and the circumstances referred to in Article are not met. 30 § 5, and the proceedings are not subject to remission as without object - Article. 105. In the event of suspension of proceedings due to the death of a party or one of the parties to the proceedings, the issuing of the relevant order shall be preceded by the determination by the public administration of the impossibility of requesting successors of the deceased party. The fact that the heirs cannot be summoned is most often due to the impossibility of establishing them. In accordance with the provision of the Article. 1027 of the Law of 23 April 1964. The Civil Code to a third party who does not claim inheritance rights, the heir can prove his rights arising from succession only with a statement of inheritance or with a registered certificate of inheritance. The caselaw points to the lack of procedural legitimacy of public administrations to initiate proceedings for the acquisition of inheritance after the deceased in the course of the proceedings of the parties. Moreover, in the vast majority of cases, there are also no grounds for the appointment of a curator of a non-observed inheritance, since according to the view expressed by the representatives of the judicature, the inheritance is considered to be excluded if the heir or heir did not actually fall into storage, management or use. The lack of interest of the heirs of the deceased party to the proceedings or the lack of legal capacity to establish the title of succession results in the absence of the possibility of a suspended administrative procedure leading to the suspension of proceedings for an indefinite period. In this case, there is a state of uncertainty, undesirable from the point of view of the law, the interests of the other parties to the proceedings, and contrary to one of the main principles of administrative procedure, the principle of speed, according to which public authorities should act in a thorough and prompt manner, using the simplest possible means to deal with it. The period of 3 years proposed in the Act to remove the obstacle that is not possible to establish heirs is long enough for us to obtain an order on the acquisition of an inheritance or an act of certificate of inheritance or to call for participation in proceedings of previously established heirs. The proposed condition limiting the use of an administrative remission institution is the demonstration by the public administration of the absence of a threat to the legitimate interest of the parties or to the social interest. The assessment would be carried out each time by a public administration body and would be subject to judicial and administrative review. Analogical regulation is contained in the Article. 182 § 1 of the Civil Procedure Code according to which the court terminates proceedings suspended at the parties' request or at the request of the heir, as well as for the reasons set out in Article. Paragraph 177 (1) (5) and (6) shall apply if the application for proceedings has not been lodged within one year of the date of the suspension order. In addition, the court will terminate the proceedings in the absence of a legal successor to a party who has lost its judicial capacity and in any event one year after the date of the decision to suspend the proceedings for this reason. The Tribunal shall also terminate the proceedings in the event of the death of the party after the expiry of years 5 from the date of the decision to suspend the proceedings for this reason. In addition, Article 2 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 provides for the application of Article 4 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013. 130 § 1 of the Law of 30 August 2002. The law on proceedings before administrative courts provides that the court shall waive suspended proceedings if the application for proceedings suspended at the request of the parties or for the reasons set out in the Article. 125, optional conditions for suspension of proceedings, Paragraph 1 (3) has not been notified within 3 years of the date of the suspension proceedings or if there is no legal successor to the party who has lost its judicial capacity, and in any event 3 years after the date of the suspension order for this reason, or in the event of the death of the party, 5 years after the date of the suspension order for this reason. We therefore propose, in view of the above arguments, to extend the possibility of withdrawing proceedings after the expiry of the three-year period of suspension of proceedings also in the event of other conditions of mandatory suspension of proceedings, i.e. the death of the legal representative of the party or the loss of legal capacity by the party or its statutory representative. The entry into force of the proposed regulation does not affect the state budget. The bill will have a very positive socio-economic impact. These chronic cases suspended ad calendas Graecas are very many and the parties have repeatedly reported this problem on the duty of Members of the Civic Platform. We believe that this provision will end many suspended administrative proceedings and will be well received by Polish citizens. On behalf of Members of the Civic Platform - because this is a project that, you can say, can bring the whole House together - I ask that all political factions in the Polish Parliament support this change. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u253	Dear and Nice Miss Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! In other words, the project leads to an extension of the possibility of withdrawing proceedings after the expiry of the three-year period of suspension of proceedings also in the event of other grounds for compulsory suspension of proceedings. This is particularly important in a situation of legitimate interest of another party opposing the termination of proceedings, which cannot be represented at any time. This raises the question of how the public administration will then make arrangements to confirm or exclude the existence of a legitimate interest of the parties. Contrary to the views of the designers, it is not possible to confirm the effectiveness of the implementation of any in-house or judicial review of a decision taken under the conditions indicated, taking into account, in particular, the fact that it cannot be effectively served by an undisclosed party. This project was part of a package prepared by the Ministry of Development of 100 changes for companies, a package of facilitations for entrepreneurs. Multiplication of the amendment of laws, and even more so of codes, is not good legislative practice, especially when it comes to changing only one rule. The lack of possibility to summon heirs is usually due to the impossibility of establishing them. In accordance with the provision of the Article. 1027 Civil Code to a third party who does not claim inheritance rights, the heir may prove his right as a result of succession only with a statement of inheritance or with a registered certificate of inheritance. The caselaw points to the lack of procedural legitimacy of public administrations to initiate proceedings for the acquisition of the estate after the deceased in the course of the proceedings. Moreover, in most cases, there is also no reason to appoint a curator for the non-observed inheritance, since according to the view expressed by the representatives of the judicature, the inheritance is considered to be excluded if the heir or heirs have not actually taken the fall into custody, management or use. The lack of interest of the heirs of the deceased party to the proceedings or the lack of legal capacity to establish the title of succession results in a lack of possibility for suspended administrative proceedings, leading to the suspension of proceedings for an infinite period. As the project applicants argue, in this case there is a state of uncertainty that is not desirable from the point of view of the legal order, the interests of the other parties to the proceedings, and which is contrary to one of the main principles of administrative conduct, namely the principle of speed of conduct whereby public administrations should act in a thorough and prompt manner, using the simplest possible means to deal with it. High Izbo! This draft amendment to the Code of Administrative Procedure aims to reduce uncertainty and eliminate the state of suspension in which many parties to proceedings are present in our country. Often for many years they cannot finally decide e.g. the legal status of the immovable property. The project is certainly an answer in the context of this problem, giving the public authorities the opportunity to withdraw their proceedings 3 years after the date of the decision to suspend proceedings due to the death of the party. The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of passing the project to the committee. We will certainly continue to support him. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u273	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill assumes that parents of children up to 10 years of age will be able to work an hour less, receiving full pay. Today, a similar legal structure applies to employees with a disability ruling. They are entitled to reduced working time while maintaining full remuneration. Today we can see that these people have trouble finding a job. What does that tell you? From complex, time-consuming methods of documenting working time, the organisation of work itself, the need to ensure continuity of work and many other aspects about which, I have the impression, the designers of this project completely forgot. The report of the Institute of Labour and Social Affairs makes it clear that instruments of the nature of rights for disabled workers, such as reduced working time, are assessed by employers who took part in the study very critically. From their point of view, they should be reduced as much as possible and actually eliminated in order to restore people with disabilities to the labour market. Have these comments been taken into account in the drafting of this law? I have doubts about that, too. The project introduces an absolute ban on the employment of an employee, a parent of a child up to the age of 10, over a period of 7 hours, thus forcing the employer to introduce specific working time schedules. It will not be possible to apply equivalent working time and shift work will lose meaning. How will employers who work in a multi-group system do this? There's no answer to that either. Will they be forced to organize separate teams of employees - parents of children under the age of 10? This is also the case with the principle of equal treatment in employment referred to in, for example, Article. 18 The Labour Code, because in this article, point 2 clearly identifies as discriminatory measures such as those which adversely affect remuneration for work or other conditions of employment among employees. For the same job one employee, working 7 hours, will receive a salary and another will have to work 8 hours. A further question is therefore raised, which is also indicated in the opinion of the Chief Labour Inspector. How would an employer verify whether the other guardian benefits from this privilege or not? What if one of the parents uses e.g. From a school leave? In such a situation, will this working parent also be entitled to a reduced working time? This question is not answered either in the explanatory memorandum or in the bill. According to the Confederation of Leviathan, this solution will have negative consequences for the work organisation process and will have a counterproductive effect. In turn, in the opinion of the Employers' Organisation of the Republic, it is important to take a responsible approach and estimate the costs of introducing the proposed solution for the state budget, the economy, but also for the labour market. If this law enters into force, employers will face the difficult task of reorganising working time in such a way that the orders are executed on time while maintaining budgetary assumptions, with fewer employees. Why did the originator of the bill decide to indicate the age of 10 years of the child as a criterion for using reduced working time? This proposal requires a number of amendments, calculations, but above all consultation with the social partners. It is worth noting that all opinions published so far, especially employers' opinions, but also the chief labour inspector, are very critical in this regard. Of course, we are aware that the idea may arouse great interest among parents who today have difficult access to educational institutions, kindergartens, nursery schools or schools, so we would like to talk about this project and consider what values and criteria to adopt to make life easier for working parents. The Labour Code already contains a provision containing restrictions on the use of the working-time schemes mentioned by me in the case of workers employed at work who exceed the maximum levels or severity of health-related factors, pregnant workers, child care workers until their 4th year of age, without their consent, their working time shall not exceed 8 hours. Will such restrictions apply to parents under 10 years of age? ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u301	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members and Members of the Posel Club Modern I have the honour to present to the High House a bill amending the law on local government, district government and local government. The citizens' initiative gives residents of municipalities, powiats and local districts an important tool to participate in the daily life of our local and regional communities. Although there are currently no legal obstacles to this issue being regulated in the statutes of individual local government units, unfortunately, local authorities do not take advantage of this possibility very often. In accordance with the rules in force, individuals may decide in a general vote, e.g. in elections or in referendums, or through the authorities of the commune, district or voivodeship, however, they do not have the right to draw up and submit draft resolutions directly and take part in the resolution process. Thanks to the citizens' initiative of resolution, residents of municipalities, powiats and voivodships will gain another, this time a direct instrument of influencing the activity of local authorities, to resolve the important problems of local communities. From the study of the Association 61, which conducted a survey in local governments, published on the portal MamPrawoWieć.pl, it follows that the citizens' resolution initiative is implemented in more than 300 local governments, but where it is implemented, it brings excellent results. Citizens' initiatives concerned civil budgets, the construction of various types of monuments, the implementation of municipal tasks, the modernisation of roads, senior cards or packages of discounts for seniors and many other local initiatives. Where the citizens' legislative initiative is being taken, the participation of residents has increased significantly, who can decide not only on the day of the election but also during the entire term of office. Therefore, it is worth introducing mandatory citizens' initiative of resolution into the law on local government, district government and local government. The second reason for preparing and accepting, I hope, this law is our meeting with the residents. They often stress that they do not have an impact on initiatives taken in their local governments, that their impact actually ends on election day. These initiatives are particularly important for all urban movements which are not represented in municipalities, powiats or voivodships. Ladies and gentlemen, I will present a few detailed provisions of this law in order to provide information on what this amendment of our law is actually about. We're adding a split. 4a. This is a change in the Local Government Act. In this chapter, which is titled "The People's Legislative Initiative," the whole procedure - from Article - is described in detail. 42a to 42e - preparation of this legal path, implementation, notification of this initiative and representation also during the debate or process of this initiative in municipal councils. For individual municipalities, we were looking for a solution that would be universal. I must tell you that where there is a citizens' initiative today, we have extremely different cases. For example, in Toruń, who had already introduced such an initiative in 1991, it was stated in the statutes that it was enough to collect 150 signatures, but we also have 6,000, 4,000. And 15,000, so sometimes it's just extremely screwed up. It is therefore difficult, suggesting this number of signatures, to find a solution that would be fully acceptable. I think this will also be the subject of our work in the parliamentary committees, but according to our proposal in the municipalities up to 5 thousand. 100 people with electoral rights should be signed under this initiative in municipalities up to 20,000. - 200 inhabitants, in municipalities over 20,000. - 300 inhabitants. In the next chapter, which amends the District Government Act, we propose that this number of signatures under the resolution initiative be 500 people. The same applies to cities on the rights of the county. Finally, in the case of voivodships and regional capitals, i.e. 18 cities that have such status, we propose that this number of signatures under such an initiative should be 1 000. As I said, if it arouses emotions, I firmly believe that in the course of further work in the parliamentary committees there will be a solution that will be satisfactory for everyone here. On behalf of the applicants, I want to declare full openness. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed solutions do not generate any additional costs, it is worth noting that they do not restrict the competence of the councils of municipalities, powiats or seymists of voivodships, which will continue to decide whether the proposed initiative deserves to be adopted and implemented. In fact, this extends the range of entities that are entitled to submit draft resolutions to the relevant bodies. I hope that this bill, which enhances citizens' rights, will gain acceptance of all the clubs and parliamentary circles represented in the Sejm, as I ask of you. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u303	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Mr. Director! I have the honour of presenting my position on behalf of the Law and Justice Club against the 1500 print project. The aim of the draft law on amending the law on local government and certain other laws according to its justification is to introduce into the Polish legal system the possibility for residents of the commune, district or voivodship of a resolution initiative. Here, it should be pointed out immediately that it is now possible to grant a resolution initiative to the inhabitants of a given local authority, as the applicant of the project draws attention to. The introduction of such solutions is permitted under the current regulations of local government laws in the statutes of the commune, district or voivodship. The possibility to include in the statutes a local government unit of regulations concerning the citizens' legislative initiative confirmed the Supreme Administrative Court in Warsaw by a judgment of 21 November 2013. Here is the short operative part of this judgment: the power to submit draft resolutions to the authorities is undoubtedly linked to the determination of the working mode of the council, which is a matter, as is clear from the article. 22 of the Municipal Government Act, belongs to the statutory matter. The right to adopt a resolution initiative for a group of residents should be linked to the subjective aspect of the definition of local government in the article. 1. It is argued here that the provision of citizens with a resolution initiative should be an important instrument of civic activity of the inhabitants giving them the opportunity to participate more in the activities for the benefit of the municipality. There is no doubt, however, that the Polish legislature allows the residents of the local government to be equipped with the possibility to submit draft resolutions and put them to the vote. There is therefore no need to settle the matter by law, as this can be regulated in the statutes of a self-government unit. In addition, the provisions of the Statute relating to the citizens' legislative initiative are often less formalised. the nature to be exercised by residents wishing to exercise such power in so far as it provides for the addition of a special chapter on the resolution initiative and is therefore beneficial to the parties concerned. Here you can give an example of the statute of Lubuskie voivodship, which only specifies who can take the legislative initiative, the conditions to be fulfilled by the draft resolution, and the requirement for applicants to indicate their representative. As a result, it could happen if the solutions proposed in the draft law came into force that the verified, readable and simple statutory provisions would be replaced by a much more extensive, less friendly procedure. There are no transitional provisions in the draft law which would allow the application of the existing statutory provisions until the adoption of new ones. [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]] In conclusion, given that it is possible to grant citizens a legislative initiative in the Statute, an act of local law, whose essential feature is flexibility to adapt the rules which standardise this matter to the local conditions, the draft submitted is negative. [[Voices from the hall: Ooh!]] In your hands, Marshal, I'm filing a motion to reject him. [[But you don't have a marshal, you're a marshal.]] So I'm putting a marshal in your hands. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u304	Thank you very much. Mr Ryszard Wilczyński will speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u305	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club vis-à-vis the parliamentary draft law amending the Municipal Government Act and some other laws, print No 1500. This project extends the catalogue of tasks belonging to the exclusive competence of the municipalities, powiats, voivodships, as regards the consideration of legislative initiatives of residents of the commune, district and voivodship. The intention is to replace the statutory regulation only with a potential and, as you can see, the often used possibility of the citizens' legislative initiative based on the statutory provisions of the local government units concerned. The project concerning laws on municipal self-government, district self-government, about voivodship self-government introduces twin chapters under the name of the initiative on the resolution of inhabitants - respectively - the commune, district and voivodship. They shall contain the requirements for notification of the resolution initiative and the rules for dealing with the draft resolution so notified. The subject of the resolution initiative is not currently part of the catalogue of key issues for the functioning of the local government in Poland, but valuablely, following the law of 24 June 1999 on the implementation of the legislative initiative by citizens, it aims to extend the scope of civic participation in the functioning of local government units. The registrations in the project of a group of residents of the commune, district, voivodship can take place with the initiative of the editorial committee. It is declared that this group of residents is the subject of this new formula of influencing the activities of the local public authority. However, it should be noted that the role of residents in implementing the resolution initiative in this submission will be limited to signing only the draft resolution in the number of 100 to 1000, depending on the size of the municipality or the type of local government unit. Further provisions are clearly the actual subject of the resolution initiative made by the Committees of the initiative of between 3 and 10 people, which represents about 1/100 of the required thresholds for support for the draft resolution. Only one member of the committee is involved in the process of the project by the board of local government units. In view of the hope of encouraging civic activity, the proposed new formula restricting the role of a group of residents in implementing the resolution initiative to signatures supporting the draft resolution seems too shallow. In the opinion of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform's subjectivity of the population group should be reflected more fully in the project, as the essence of the resolution initiative is the solution to the problem, except that the initiation of action from the authorities of the local government unit is shifted to citizens, members of the local government community. In our view, it is worth accepting the solution that the population group operates through the resolution initiative committee. It is not necessary to specify the size of this group, as it has a role to support the initiative as outlined in the draft. It is worth considering that the draft resolution and the process of adopting it include real participatory formulas, e.g. the requirement to hold an open meeting to present the draft on which the resolution initiative committee is attached and to have a public hearing organised by the chairman of the board in the course of the work on the draft. It should be noted that the draft law also contains controversial provisions on criminal sanctions - a sentence of imprisonment from 3 months to 5 years - for interfering with the implementation of the legislative initiative or, by abuse of the relationship of dependence, affecting its execution, which seems unnecessary. In conclusion, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club sees the obvious advantages of the proposal, which extends the scope of civic participation and creates an additional instrument for influencing the functioning of the local government. For this reason, he has the support of our club. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

PLN up to the amount of 100,000. zł. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u337	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! According to data from the Ministry of Justice, for more than 15 years there has been an increase in the number of offences affecting animal health and life, which is worse, an increasing number of such offences committed in a qualified form each year, i.e. with special cruelty. Currently, every year there is a valid conviction of about 600 perpetrators who kill animals, abuse animals, and persons who violate the rules applicable to the slaughter of animals. It should also be pointed out that the revision of the existing rules is supported by the fact that the limits of criminal liability for the killing of animals and the abuse of animals differ significantly from the level of penalties that may be imposed on perpetrators, for example. theft of an animal, i.e. 3 months to 5 years imprisonment or animal hunting during a protective period, i.e. Up to 5 years of imprisonment. The current criminal risk for killing an animal is 2 years, which is a grossly low penalty compared to other penalties. crime and far too mild, given that it protects the highest good, that is, animal life. The project has been prepared to address the problems that exist. Thus, in the case of animal killing and animal abuse, the project provides for an increase in the sentence of 2 years imprisonment to 3 years, while in the case of the perpetrator with special cruelty - from 3 years to 5 years imprisonment. Another of the demands of this project is the introduction of a mandatory decision on animal protection in order to convict the perpetrator of a crime against animal life and health. The introduction of such additional sanctions will increase awareness on the part of the perpetrators as to the reprehensibility of their conduct and will also reduce the number of cases of the perpetrators returning to the crime. Undoubtedly, the introduction of this obligation in the perspective will have a positive impact on the formation of the public's legal awareness of the criminal protection of animals. Importantly, the funds of the institutions will be transferred to various types of animal protection organisations, which will contribute to their effectiveness. High Izbo, in the current state of law, is extremely rare and, at a very low rate, most often not more than 1 000. zł. Extensive changes in criminal measures in the form of a ban on the possession of any animal or specific category of animal and the prohibition on the establishment and pursuit of the profession of animals against the perpetrators of such offences are also commendable. Those prohibitions will operate by analogy with Article. 41 The Penal Code, which provides the possibility to prohibit the occupation of a particular position, the pursuit of a particular profession and the pursuit of a business activity. Both of these criminal measures will be imposed on perpetrators who committed a criminal offence with particular cruelty against animal life and health. High Izbo! So far, there have also been no specific regulations allowing effective enforcement from those convicted of crimes against the protection of animals of compliance with criminal measures, so that the banned ban was not translated into reality. The proposal also introduces a very necessary regulation on this issue. The last amendment is to clarify the wording of the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 2 point 18 of the Animal Protection Act, which provides criminal liability for persons who transport or hold live fish for sale or without sufficient water for breathing. High Izbo! The continuous increase in animal crime, including the increase in the number of acts committed with particular cruelty, is an undeniable basis for concluding that the current criminal rules do not fulfil their task. The Law and Justice Club supports this government bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Currently, the law states that by bullying animals, it is necessary to mean inflicting or knowingly allowing pain or suffering, and in particular point 18: transporting live fish or keeping them for sale without sufficient water to prevent breathing. Projectors want to change this to: transporting live fish or keeping them in conditions that prevent breathing. As a result, ensuring only the exchange of gas through the skin in fish will be sufficient to be able to transport these animals in accordance with the regulations. Let me quote a passage from the letter sent to you by e-mail as part of the citizens' protest: The Supreme Court in its judgment of 13 December 2016 pointed out that carps require protection like other vertebrate animals, stating that the natural environment of fish is the aquatic environment, and therefore the principle should be to transport, retain and transfer fish in an aquatic environment, i.e. in such a way as to ensure their proper living conditions, i.e. the possibility of their existence in accordance with their species needs. On the other hand, the article. 1 paragraph 1 This law clearly speaks of the status of an animal who, as a living being capable of suffering, is not a thing. A man owes him respect, protection and care. The law is not limited to listing the catalogue of prohibited acts, it regulates the attitude towards animals based on humane treatment and rights, not just the response to possible situations. Therefore, this provision requires further work in the committee in order to formulate it so as to prevent too broad interpretation and to be consistent with the intentions that we can read about in the explanatory memorandum of your project. The Animal Protection Act has required changes for a long time, both in terms of the relationship between social values and the legal normative system... What is worth pointing out on this occasion is that criminal legislation should propose the introduction of an element of corrective justice, which is socially useful work for a social organisation whose statutory objective is to protect animals, rather than a fine or alongside it. The purpose of justice here is not to retaliate, but to make amends to damage or harm. The use of remedies reduces the risk of a similar offence in the future and promotes social rehabilitation. In the government project, a provision was made that, in the event of a conviction for an animal offence, the court would have to declare an animal protection purpose on a case-by-case basis. At present, this is not mandatory. In addition, the provisions proposed in the draft aim to increase penalties for the killing and abuse of animals, the introduction of a judicial ban on the pursuit of professions, activities or activities requiring authorisation of the use of animals or the impact on them of special cruelty to them, and the prohibition on the possession of animals by those persons. ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u353	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Animal Protection Act and the Penal Code Act. This amendment aims to increase the protection of animals. To this end, applicants propose inter alia. increase maximum penalties for the killing of animals and the abuse of animals from 2 to 3 years in prison, and in cases of special cruelty up to 5 years. In addition, for any conviction, the court will be required to give a judgment on the purpose of protecting animals, which may be of a maximum amount of 100,000. zł. It is a good idea to increase the capacity to rule on high claims, but whether it is such a restriction of the freedom of the court to rule by imposing a mandatory imposition, I am already in doubt. Similarly, increasing the upper limit of punishment, without ensuring its inevitability, which I would focus more on, raises my doubts. In addition, the proposal provides for a compulsory ban on the holding of animals and a ban on the occupation of animals for persons who have treated animals with particular cruelty in the past, while extending the period for which such a ban may be issued from 10 to 15 years. I do not fully agree with the claim of the projectors that merely tightening up legislation, acting as an effective preventive measure, will deter potential perpetrators from violence against animals and will translate into a reduction in the number of offences committed in this category. I mentioned earlier that, in my opinion, the inevitability of punishment, not its severity, is a better deterrent. However, given the data and statistics cited in the explanatory memorandum, there is no doubt that increased action in this area is necessary and in my view this is in line with social expectations. In my view, one of the more effective solutions proposed in the amendment is to increase the level of court-mandatory formations. The use of additional funds for animal protection purposes may not only prove to be an effective educational measure, it will certainly be realistic and positive in terms of opportunities for helping non-governmental organisations. The Free and Solidarnia Circle, despite its doubts, is optimistic about the assumptions of this amendment and will vote in favour of further proceding it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-05-10-sejm-41-1.u397	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I'd like to introduce to you the figure of a young hero from Ciechanowska - Adasia Rzewuski. On the southern side of the drug forest there was a farm called Maliniak. There was a family of poor Rzewuskie aristocrats. They came all the way from Fastov near Kiev in 1942. The father of the family Władysław Rzewuski grew several acres of low sandy soil on this Maliniak and raised four of his children in a patriotic family tradition. Direct proximity to the forest, considerable distance from frequented roads and larger human clusters, as well as the patriotic attitude of the host led to the establishment of a conspiracy centre here. Here he established his headquarters of an inspector of the Armed Fighting Union, and then the Home Army, comprising in the last phase of his activity three circuits: Ciechanowski, Mławski and Działdowski. The whole family of Rzewuski became involved in conspiracy work. The youngest, 17-year-old Adaś named himself Storm. Their house became a meeting place and a meeting place. This was where the news and all sorts of conspiracy materials were transmitted. On the last day of March 1944, Jan Barański ps. The worm received an order from Inspector Benicki, who was sent to the contact point in the village of Bolewo four packages containing filipinos, newspapers, orders and clothing. A boy named Adasia Rzewuski was assigned to help him. Armed with two guns, a nine-shot parabellum, they were going to make sure they were okay. They ignored the rules of conspiracy. They walked together, side by side, instead of some distance, just keeping eye contact. They did not notice the quietly approaching sleighs carried by a Polish cartman with two policemen. They heard a sharp whistle calling them to stop. When asked how they would explain themselves, Baranski answered: with guns. He put his hand in the pocket of his coat and ripped out the safety gun. However, the Germans warned him and the shooting took place. At the same time, two shots were fired: the gendarme hit Adasia, the Baranski fatally struck the German. The last rounds of the Baranski were directed towards the second policeman fleeing into the woods. When he ran out of bullets, he ran up to Adaz and pulled the boy's arm. This one didn't say anything. Then the Baranski jumped behind the nearest tree and fleeing round and round, ran to Maliniak to inform the Rzewuski about what had happened. By protecting herself from the German threat, the family left home and went wandering until the end of the war. Meanwhile, in the forest, the remaining gendarmes with the help of a van loaded his dead friend on the sleigh and approached Adasia. The boy who regained consciousness was groaning in pain. They and the truck helped him get up and get in the sled. After taking the physical evidence, they headed for the drug. Adaś considered his position all the way, meditated on his conduct, and perhaps at that time, he decided his fate on his own in the face of mortal danger. After his initial interrogation, the boy was locked up in local custody under strict guard. He was too valuable a prey. She was summoned to him by a midwife to look at her wounds. In such suffering, Adas died. On the site of the skirmish in 1969, the Ciechanov Land Lovers' Society set up a field boulder with the inscription: "Here, on March 31, 1944, he was wounded in the fight of Adaś Rzewuski ps. Storm. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Told by the Gestapo, he died the day after the torture in Lekowo. Thank you. [[Applause]]
